ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u1	Good morning, the session is open. The next item is the Official Gazette of Parliament, which is not to be read. Mr. Antonio Mouriño Villar has justified his absence for a health problem. And we're on the agenda. Mr President-in-Office of the Council of Galicia, at the request of the GGs. PP. of In Marea, the Galician Socialists and the Galego Nationalist Block, to account for the results of the Conference of Presidents
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u2	Mr. President of the Galician Council has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u3	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen. We greet the boys and girls of the Institute of Secondary Education of Brión, who honor us with their presence. Ladies and gentlemen, I come to this Parliament to transfer to you the content, development and results of the Conference of Presidents that we have just celebrated just a week ago, last Tuesday, in the Senate, in Madrid. As you know, this is the third time I have the opportunity to attend a conference of presidents as president of the community. And the first thing I wanted to pass on to you is that on this occasion, in my opinion, this conference has served to reflect on the importance of working together from the legitimate discrepancy but with due loyalty and height of sight. For this reason, I think we are at the first opportunity to see that this conference can have a real benefit for Spain for autonomy. Beyond the concrete agreements we've reached, and in which I'll stop later, let me convey my satisfaction to them for a triple fact. First of all, because the Conference of Presidents had been held, as we have been complaining since Galicia, and that it had been in a climate of cordiality. Secondly, because we will have instruments to monitor and follow up the conference itself to ensure that the agreements reached are developed in time and form. And thirdly, because we have been able to pave the way for an extraordinary forum so far, the Conference of Presidents — only five conferences of presidents since the 1978 Constitution — to become a body that works in a usual way for the common placement of matters that affect us. From every point of view, any one of you can see that the shared will of all of us who participate in that conference is to set up a new framework in which autonomous communities can first debate together the concerns; secondly, to make our efforts closer so that the improvement of the functioning of public administrations is made with a whole vision; and finally, as a result of the previous ones, to be more useful to citizens. That was the maximum that led the preparatory work, a key role for the meeting to be truly useful. It was also the peak who presided over the meeting, in which we all spoke, and heard us without cuts. I hope it will be the best in the future too so that this first important but insufficient step will be completed and implemented in agreements, even in state agreements, on fundamental issues for all citizens. Many of you will remember that more than seven years ago — some of you, at least — appeared the same as today to account for the presidents' conference in December 2009. Then I said that, due to a lack of preparation, their usefulness had been compromised. Well, on the contrary, I think I can say on this occasion that if there was an interesting previous work and an effort of all to reach agreements and to make those agreements something productive. We can't lose sight, in any case, of what presidents' conferences are supposed to be. It can never be said that these meetings will come out with definitive solutions to the problems. But if I think, at least on this occasion, if we can talk about a clear intention to take steps forward to face important challenges, challenges that we have before us. And what is perhaps most remarkable is that it showed an intention to do so, and to do it together, dialogue between all of us and for all of us. Because, indeed, the agreements reached and those that can derive further will be fruit of the comings of all those who want to participate and thought for the well-being and prosperity of all Spaniards, living where they live. If Galicia has always defended the use of the Conference of Presidents more frequently, it is precisely because in our view, and as has been demonstrated, it can be an axile body to address matters that require the consensus of the Central Government and the consensus of self-government. No unilateral policy, much less if it is medium-light and reserved, nor any unilateral policy, whether it is medium-light or reserved, must replace the common forums to address the priorities that are of all. That's why, ladies and gentlemen, the conference was right for now. That's why we'll keep right on walking in the unit that came out of it. And that's why those who didn't want to go to her will have to explain why they're self-exclusive of the possibility of enriching the important agreements for the lives of the citizens they represent. And I say important agreements because finally there came out a total of 11 agreements on various subjects that they all have a special transcendence for the exercise of our self-government, and to offer better services and better opportunities to citizens. There we have talked about, among other things, the following: self-financing, how to finance dependence and how to often analyse the impact of health spending on total expenditure of autonomous communities; how to go towards an educational model agreed, durable and effective; how to improve employment opportunities; how to improve relations with the European Union and the presence of autonomous communities in the formation of the Central Government will in the forums of the Union; of the refugee crisis; of gender violence; how to help families with less resources in access to electricity or through the social card; and, of course, there talked about a key theme to ensure our future, a key theme for Spain, but, of course, a key theme for the proposal of autonomous communities and especially the Autonomous Community Galga, there talked about the demographic evolution of the whole. In all of them I think you've come to interesting agreements. And I believe that, from Galicia's point of view, we can say that it was first achieved that key issues such as self-financing, health expenditure and demography, which we wanted before the conference were dealt with, were during the conference at the top of the table and formed a fundamental part of the final agreements. And, secondly, Galicia has achieved that it was treated with a wide range of aims and constructive spirit by all there present the health, dependence and demographic problems of Galicia. Before I comment on you in more detail on all the agreements we have reached, let me explain to you that I will leave to the end the agreements that I consider most relevant at this moment. I mean, of course, three, I mean education, autonomic funding and the demographic challenge. There were seven other proposed opening agreements referred to, as I have just concluded, firstly, the functioning of the Conference of Presidents; secondly, civil protection; thirdly, relations with the Union; fourthly, the unity of the market, digital administration and public employment; fifthly, the social bond as a guarantee of supply to families with less resources; sixthly, the social card; and seventhly, employment. And in addition to those established at the beginning, we could include two new issues of relevance: the fight against gender-based violence and the paragraph referring to the situation of refugees. I am therefore now offering you the assessment that we have made since the Government with regard to each of these agreements. Firstly, the Agreement on the amendment of the Rules of Procedure of the Conference of Presidents, which refers mainly to the amendment of Article 8 to add two functions of the preparatory committee of that Conference. First, take the necessary steps to ensure compliance with the agreements, including six-month follow-up meetings. The second is to coordinate the actions of the sectoral conferences and the correct implementation of the Law on the Jurisdiction of the Public Sector in relation to inter-administrative relations. We remember at the beginning, but I insist again that we positively value this plugin, because it is supposed to give the Conference a greater operational capacity and ensure that its agreements will be monitored so that they can be evaluated. Finally, the guarantee that every six months there will be a follow-up to the agreements and an annual conference of presidents at least. In the investment speech I made in this House less than a hundred days ago, I myself said that it would be a step forward to make the Conference of Presidents from being something exceptional and almost symbolic to becoming something ordinary and something effective. That is why we understand that this agreement can contribute to the fact that both the General Administration of the State and the autonomous communities involve us more in the elaboration of realistic agreements, feasible agreements and, above all, agreements that have a real impact on the ordinary life of people. Since Galicia, recalling that President Fraga was the first one who spoke of the need for this conference of presidents, we can only be satisfied with the steps taken to ensure that this conference has a vocation of continuity, six-monthly follow-up and annual periodicity. Secondly, we have reached an agreement on civil protection. We believe that this agreement will serve to improve coordination among public administrations in emergency care, and that is why we agreed on the constitution of the National Council for Civil Protection before 31 March and the adoption of measures for the development of joint work within the Council. As stated in the agreement, coordination and cooperation should not be an objective, but a guarantee of the quality of response to emergency situations. So we welcome any step that goes in the line of trying to achieve maximum coordination, maximum flexibility, maximum efficiency and effectiveness in civil protection actions in the state as a whole. Thirdly, relations with the European Union, as all your lords know, are key to the life of the autonomous communities and to our relationship with the General Administration of the State. For so many matters in which European regulation directly affects the Greek homes, for all of the Greeks, we must develop coordination in the position of the central government in the European institutions from autonomous governments. And much more in this context where European construction is questioned by some. There we have, for example, the challenge of Brexit, which will be one before and after in our relations and in the configuration of the European Union itself. And in that process the voice of autonomous communities must be heard and valued. In this sense, I think it is a step forward in last Tuesday's agreement, so it is decided to convene the European Union-related conference in the first quarter of 2017 with four objectives. The first is to review and update the functioning and organisation of the European Union-related conference itself. Secondly, to assess coordination in the preparation of the Councils of the Union. Thirdly, to encourage greater participation of sectoral conferences in setting up the Spanish position before the Community institutions. And finally, to strengthen coordination between the State Administration and the autonomous communities in the processes of negotiation, programming, management and execution of European funds, which are important, essential, for Galicia. Since the creation of this conference, Galicia has been actively involved in all its organs and has been one of the driving communities of many of the proposals. That is why everything that means moving forward, reviewing and updating the functioning and organization of the conference for European affairs is also positively appreciated by the Government. As I pointed out earlier, in this agreement we decided to incorporate a new point on the situation of refugees, further aggravated by the harshness of this winter in Europe, and for what we are going to promote a coordinated emergency aid plan through the cooperation or development departments of all public administrations. Fourthly, the important agreement on market unity, digital administration, inter-administrative mobility and recovery of replacement rates for public employees in key administrative areas. As I said at the outset, we are a country without borders and without barriers, and therefore agreements aimed at strengthening this idea, as is the paragraph referred to in the market unit, must always be well received. In the digital administration, we believe that it is also a step forward that recognizes the need to work more in promoting the use of shared services and tools with the aim of agitating and simplifying citizens' relations with their public administrations. In the same sense of eliminating barriers, we believe it is also desirable to continue to move forward to facilitate the mobility of public workers in all public administrations that make up the Spanish State. Through the principle of mobility, it is about achieving, on the one hand, better use of the human resources of the public sector and, on the other, that public employees can change both job and job and location, and thus facilitate geographic mobility throughout the national territory. It's about working now to advance on issues such as reciprocity to reinforce this bet by the mobility of public employees in all administrations. In public employment, we also agreed to analyze the replacement rates according to the needs of the priority sectors of the autonomous communities — in which they undoubtedly merge all their lordships — and health, education, social services, security and emergencies. We welcome the possibility of abolishing the replacement rates for public personnel at the moment. We remember that the replacement rates were reduced to 10% during the previous socialist government, and it was a measure of obligation to comply with all Spanish public administrations. Subsequently, this limitation has expanded to 30% in education, health and social services. Subsequently, it also increased in essential public services up to 50% —replacement capacity in 2015 — and 100% in the past year, 2016. The Union has fulfilled, as it cannot be otherwise, the laws of the State, and much more the basic laws of thanking all administrations. However, I remind you again that the Board has always maintained that at a time of economic recovery it is possible to remove the replacement rates so that administrations can cope with the ageing of their staff in certain public services. But even though it is a fact that we are starting a period of economic recovery — which I hope will be consolidated — the reality is that there are still many families with serious difficulties. That is why the Conference of Presidents also reached two agreements that directly affect these families: the case of the social bond and the case of the social card. With regard to the social bond and the guarantee of electricity supply, it was agreed that autonomous communities have an effective participation in the regulatory development of Royal Decree Law 7/2016, so the mechanism for financing the cost of the social bond and other measures to protect the vulnerable consumer is regulated. In addition, the agreement envisages the collaboration of the General Administration of the State and the electricity sector in financing the measures put in place by the autonomous communities to help vulnerable consumers. We believe that this collaboration between different administrations and that reference to how to support all families is very necessary, and that is why we properly value this agreement. In fact, in Galicia, as you know, ladies and gentlemen, we've been working on this line for some time. We are pioneers in activating measures to respond to the needs of the most vulnerable consumers. Since 2014 we have a social electric ticket, which contributes to the payment of the bill of light with up to 300 euros per year per family. Last year we added an automatic mechanism to prevent the cutting of light by default to families with less resources, which in 2017, of course, will be maintained and which, in addition, extends to natural gas. In total, since 2014 we have helped over 6,000 families, over 6,000 homes. Also related to people with more needs we want to move forward in the configuration of the future social card, which will allow access to the register of public social benefits so that the benefits that any person receives from any public service can be known at all times. That is why the agreement is that within three months we will create a working group with participation from the State Administration, the Autonomous Communities and the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, which will also incorporate the approaches of the third sector of social action, for the design and implementation of the social card. I'm sure Galicia will be able to draw closer to its experience in the development of the single social history, which, as you know, is beginning to be implanted in our autonomous community since last summer. I believe it speaks well of the development of the Conference of Presidents in reaching agreement on a subject which has not been subjected to the pre-meeting work. A matter like masculine violence, gender violence, could not be left out of a document that collected the opinions and concerns of presidents and autonomic presidents. That's why I think the next monographic debate to hold in the Senate, as indicated in the agreement, will be a good opportunity to continue insisting on the need to work together to achieve a pact that will contribute to ridding our society of this problem. Finally, before I stop at the three themes which I consider to be key to the meeting, I will no doubt refer to another of great importance: employment. The conference spoke more specifically about active employment policies and how they deal with them at a time when it is necessary to consolidate the path taken over the past two years by creating a million jobs. Without a doubt, the agreement reached could serve as an impetus for renewing the Spanish Employment Activation Strategy 2017-2020, paying attention to creating more jobs and, above all, more more jobs of higher purchasing power. It is good that there is a need to improve the coordination and information exchange instruments within the framework of the National Employment System by advancing to this end the evaluation of active employment policies based on results and their planning in the first quarter of 2017 as well. It reinforces the commitment to further improving the vocational training system, in particular dual training, by promoting the participation of autonomous communities in the management of available resources. And it is also established in the agreement that the priority orientation of services and programmes in this area should, firstly, focus on improving the employability of young people, young people; secondly, on employability of other vulnerable groups — more than 65, long-term unemployed, people at risk of social exclusion and people with functional diversity. In Galicia we are clear that active employment policies can and should be strengthened, and for that reason, for this 2017, employment and training budgets are 16.4% lower than last year 2016. In addition, with a clear commitment, firstly, to creating more stable and quality jobs; secondly, by training and guidance focused on the needs of the labour market, with particular attention to the development of industry 4.0. In particular, ladies and gentlemen, in Galicia we have set the challenge of creating 20,000 new jobs this year 2017, after obtaining the Greek society that 80,000 people have left the unemployment lists since the beginning of the previous legislation 2012-2016. Now I'm going to comment on the three things I think this conference can be remembered if we're able to complete the work that we started. I refer to the pact for education, demographic challenge and self-financing. As for the education pact, I think it's good to remember that our education system has important weaknesses. Nor does anyone argue that throughout the democratic period all the organic laws involved — all driven by governments of the Socialist Party, except LOMCE — never broke a general agreement, nor those promoted by the People's Party. And, therefore, the fact that there now seems to be such a will to achieve a social and political pact in education is a source of satisfaction. It opens a new stage, a new stage of dialogue with all members of the educational community, with the sole aim of realizing a pact for which citizens are claiming political responsibility. In this understanding that should be sought, it is good — in my opinion it is not good, it is essential — that the opinion of the autonomous communities should not stand aside. And it is therefore crucial that a monographic debate on education had been agreed at the General Commission of Autonomous Communities in the Senate, a debate to incorporate the diagnosis and approach of autonomous communities into the work of the social and political pact for education. In this context, Galicia is in a position to approach its experience in aspects in which I think we can approach. I am referring, for example, to the implementation of multilingual teaching and the balance of co-official languages. I'm referring to the approach of coexistence in educational centers. With the Law of coexistence and participation of the educational community, adopted by this Parliament, with the protocols of action, we were the first to regulate the prevention, detection and treatment of harassment and cyber harassment, as well as with the first Equal Education Plan. In addition, I believe that we can also approach the empowerment of vocational training, with the offer of new cycles adapted to the economic and social reality of each area, with the bet on integrated vocational training centres and the development of modalities such as Dual Vocational Training, Basic Vocational Training and Distance Vocational Training. I think we can also approach the specialization of educational supply in university fields, which is what we are currently working on in Spain as a pioneering autonomous community. And we can also come to funding for the results of the university system, which is currently in force in Galicia. Ladies and gentlemen, we must try a state pact, a state pact for education, and I hope that we will take this opportunity on this occasion. Just as setting up a quality education is key to our future, it's also key to facing the challenge that demographic evolution is posing to all of us, of course in Galicia, but also in Spain and most of the European Union. It is a challenge marked by a progressive ageing of the population that requires a configuration of population pyramids with very narrow bases that compromise the viability of the entire welfare system. This is one of the concerns that Galicia wanted to bring to the Conference of Presidents, and I believe that the agreement reached takes the first step, which is not a minor one, to recognize the transcendence of the demographic problem and to start talking about how to deal with it in a coordinated manner, for the first time, throughout the country. As you know and as we have many times said in this House, that problem is not a new problem, it comes from the last century, from the 1980s. It's not a new problem and therefore it won't be solved in the short term because it's been managing, as I say, for decades. But it is also now beginning to become a reality in many of the autonomous Spanish communities and in many of the States of the Union. In Galicia, in particular, we went from 40,000 births in the mid-1970s to a minimum of 18,500 in 1998, and are now around 20,000. It went from a percentage of over 65 years of age to 16.5% in 1991 to 24% today. But there is no country in Europe that does not now have a fertility rate that guarantees generational relief. Only France approaches it, 2.1; Spain, 1.32; Germany, 1.47, Italy, 1.37; United Kingdom, 1.82; Sweden, 1.88. In Galicia, we have talked about this priority, and this needs individual and collective work, and that's why we work here and work with our partners from other autonomous communities and we create the Spanish Regional Forum with Demographic Challenges. So the fact that we now remember that a national strategy against the demographic challenge is going to be developed, that this issue had focused much of the conference and is now on the priority political agenda of the whole nation, as the President of the Central Government pointed out in his press round, has great value for us. It is worth the fact that the agreement pointed out that this national strategy should be approved this year 2017, and it is also worth all the work we have done on it, a work that will serve as a basis — so explicitly the agreement — to formulate the national strategy against the demographic challenge. And it is worth it because we agree that, as far as we are able, we all defend before the European Union authorities that Community policies have to respond to demographic challenges. Finally, with regard to self-financing, we all agree that this is also a key issue to be addressed without delay, and with the most constructive attitude possible. We have examples of what worked out, and we are actually experiencing the consequences of a model that was born out of multilateral negotiation, which was designed to meet the specific political needs of an autonomous community and its relationship with the central government of turn. I think we all share that we must not fall back into the mistakes of the past. Here we know that when we talk about self-financing, we talk about resources needed to provide services, services that have some costs. And that's what we've all been there trying to talk about. Everyone, I insist on everyone, because this is not a problem that will be solved by bilateral dialogues. And, as I said earlier, those who haven't been invited to join this joint dialogue. The model will be all or not. And as we had made it clear that the model must collect the actual cost of services, the debate must be more of the needs of people than of land planning. What it's about is how services arrive equally to the boys, the elders, the families, the people who live in the city, those who live in small villages or those who live in the villages. At last Tuesday's meeting we had an opportunity to talk about it, and talk about how Galicia needs to be taken into account its peculiarities. So I could remind the rest of the presidents of the following data. The population over 65 in Galicia is 5% more than the average in Spain: 24% in Galicia and 19% in Spain. We have 6% of the population, but 14% of the total number of health centres and 8% of the total number of schools. School transport in Galicia is almost 4 times more expensive than the average of the Spanish population, and can cost more than 4,000 euros per student on occasion. I have also had occasion to remind the rest of the presidents that the system must be stable, sustainable and progressive — as I have demonstrated in this Parliament — stable to overcome the continuing situation of provisionality, sustainable so that it will not end up being frustrating by unreality, and progressive in that it will allow solidarity transvasion from autonomous communities with more resources to other autonomous communities with less income. And of course I also had an opportunity to remind them of the importance of thinking about the impact of health spending on the total expenditure of autonomous communities. Health expenditure represents between 37% and 40% of the non-financial expenditure of the autonomous communities. In autonomous communities such as Madrid is already 42%. Pharmaceutical spending in Galicia is three times as much as spending in universities. In Galicia, 65-year-olds account for almost 50% of hospital stays, and 65-year-olds account for 62% of total pharmacy expenses. From Galicia we have to show some satisfaction for the fact, first, that we should be heard and, according to that our approaches were accepted. The good reception by the unanimous practice of all autonomous presidents in their interventions made a paragraph to Galicia's proposal, which is included in the agreement, where it is obliged to study the impact of health expenditure on the expenditure set of autonomous communities in order to compute a financing system. It is not only about how we ensure the maintenance of the health system that we now have — which, of course, too — but about how we can face the challenge of further improving, how we can face the challenge of further expanding, continuing to modernize, improve hospitals, continue to plan hospital directors, continue to purchase technology, follow up and complete the map of health centres in Galicia; finally, we can also incorporate new advances and new benefits and new diagnostic techniques. As I have just said, there will only be a new model of funding if there is agreement. And I add: there will only be agreement if public health is addressed with relevance, rigor and seriousness that is needed. I would like to highlight another aspect of the agreement, so I think we can all show satisfaction. I am referring to the fact that the financing agreement has to raise the need to analyse the situation of concern to the unit and its financing. It's a particularly sensitive matter so that we all get involved in finding solutions to the difficulties that the current model may be generating. And finally the agreement also envisages the commitment to analyse how fiscal co-responsibility is being exercised in the whole of the autonomous communities that we make up Spain. In short, in the iodine of the new financing model, the playing field is marked, and now much work remains to be done. First of all, a committee of experts should be set up and set up within a maximum of one month by which the State proposes and one by each autonomous community. This committee will have to carry out the necessary analyses for the subsequent formulation of a new model of autonomous financing, based on the principles of LOFCA, the principles of solidarity, adequacy, equity, transparency and fiscal co-responsibility, ensuring that citizens have access to public services in accordance with the actual cost of public services and making a special fincapé in health spending policies. Here's the milestone for the experts. And once the experts prepare their report, this analysis will go up to the Fiscal and Financial Policy Council. The goal is to have a new financing model at the end of 2017; that is yes, a new consensus funding model. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, the balance of the Conference of Presidents is positive and hopeful. Why? Because it's done an important previous job with the search for agreements always in mind, because there was an open and unrestricted dialogue in which we all participate, and because there's a desire for agreements to be put into practice. It is positive and hopeful from the point of Galicia, especially because all the issues we have raised have been specifically collected, those we are interested in, or we would say that we are most interested in: demographics and health expenditure. But this positive appraisal should not make us forget the reality of what this conference of presidents is. After almost five years of holding, this conference of presidents is no more than a starting point. We hope it will be a starting point for a new time of understanding, but we are aware that there is a long work ahead. It won't be easy, but I'd like to believe that the willingness to agree that I had the opportunity to live a week ago by everyone we were there was not just a pose, and we can achieve the goals marked if we continue the scope and content of the attitudes shown there and concrete in the agreements. I do not want to forget that in the development of last Tuesday's agreements it would be very interesting to have this Parliament's backing. Our will is that which I have manifested since this very stage in the investment debate. We are open to dialogue and agreement with the House's groups on all matters discussed at the conference, and that is why I want to transfer four concrete commitments to them. The first is that in the wake of autonomous funding the first step we have to take is to appoint the expert or expert representing Galicia in the commission. I think it would be good if this expert's appointment would stir up a high degree of consensus in the House, it would be a good way to begin to profile a strengthened position of country in the negotiation process of the new financing model. To this end, the Fairy Councillor will contact the next few days with parliamentary groups to establish a dialogue on the name of the expert or expert most suitable to perform this task. Our will is to make this person's choice, which since the first meeting in Galicia will be proposed — and we have to do so before a month — to be part of this committee of experts has the largest backing of the House. The second proposal, in relation to the demographic challenge, ladies and gentlemen, follows the offer I myself made at the end of last month. We are open to modify the decree that regulates the composition of the Galego Demographic Dynamization Observatory. In this observatory there is already the Galaga Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, trade unions, entrepreneurs, universities and experts in this field. It is now about adapting it so that representatives of parliamentary groups can be incorporated. An observatory which has as its main task to achieve a political, social and economic pact that translates into a Welsh law of demographic impulse with the greatest possible consensus. It would be good if the proposals we can make in this body were the proposals that we brought to the national strategy that the presidents agreed to have adopted in 2017. Third proposal. As for the agreement to consolidate the Conference of Presidents as a useful tool, we logically assume a commitment to information and transparency. Whenever there is a meeting of the Conference of Presidents — which under the agreement will be annual — the President of the European Union will have to attend automatically to ascertain the scope and content of the Conference. And when a follow-up committee meeting is held to assess the state of the agreements — which they know must be semi-annual — the Vice-President of the Board will also appear in the House to keep the groups of progress being recorded. To these three commitments I can add an additional commitment from the People's Group in relation to the agreement on gender violence, since this afternoon we will be able to discuss an amendment that we tabled to the BNG motion in this regard, a proposal to be able to raise a single and joint position of all the groups of the House to the meeting of the Senate's General Commission of Autonomous Communities which agreed to convene after the Conference of Presidents. Ladies and gentlemen, finally, so that the first step that this Conference of Presidents is not the last one, on our part there will always be, firstly, a vigilant and demanding attitude for the agreements reached to be fulfilled, and, secondly, a negotiating will with the Central Government, with the rest of the autonomous communities, and, of course, with the groups of this House to seek out the best for Galicia, which is to find the best for the Greeks and the Greeks, who are the ones we owe. Thank you very much for your attention. I'll be at your disposal.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u4	Thank you, Mr. President. We turn around the parliamentary groups. We start with the Galego Nationalist Block Parliamentary Group, and you have the floor of Mrs. Pontón.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u5	Thank you very much, Mr President of Parliament. President-in-Office of the Council of Galicia, first of all, I believe it is good that there is finally a government presence. I also believe it is good that this first appearance is the President and that he has accepted a request from the opposition. I think this is an anecdote for now. What we would like, from the BNG Parliamentary Group, is that we have introduced a new practice in this Galician Parliament, and that not only the President but also counsellors and advisors have come to the opposition's request. I'm not saying it always has to be at the request of the opposition, but it's not exceptional. Because I think that would be a symptom of respect for Parliament, that we are representatives of the people, and therefore one of respect for the Greeks and the Greeks and a symptom of democratic health, which I believe this Parliament misses a lot. We also believe, Mr President, that it would be normal for us to know in advance what position the German Government will defend on behalf of the country. I think it would be more logical if the country's issues had been realized before, and I hadn't just come here to realize the facts we've had right now, where the German Government didn't know or know exactly what the proposals were, which were really their contributions to that conference of presidents. And the truth is, it's all pretty opaque. We would like to start by telling you, Mr President, that we believe this Conference of Autonomous Presidents was really a bluff. And it was a bluff because you yourself had to recognize that those who marked the political path weren't present, were absent. Who has been marking the political agenda of the State was precisely the presidents of the nations that exist in the Spanish State who exercise as such and who mark a path of their own for the benefit of their peoples, of being able to move forward and of being able to make progress. Therefore, we believe that, more than in an institutional act, at that conference we are like a second part of those bipartisan agreements between the People's Party and the Socialist Party within the framework of this operation that have just been launched in this legislation that we can call "operation to save the regime and the status quo of 78." And many of the proposals that you have reported to us about are a new re-centralisation in areas such as public procurement, such as the fight against gender-based violence or in relation to education. It is true that federal systems, including the state of autonomy, have two aspects. The first is the differential aspects, the different preferences of citizens living in each territory. These specificities are materialized in autonomous legislation, and this leads to a certain competitiveness which, from an institutional point of view, is good for comparing management models and that communities can, in turn, improve their forms of management, but also have an aspect which is the cooperative, which is to coordinate — for the common good, to achieve common objectives — the work of different administrations. In this respect, the Conference of Presidents is essential, because Spain is the most decentralized country — with the exception of Switzerland — of all Europe, and has made a great deal of progress in the competitive aspects: we have legislative capacity, which does not have the Länder; we have fiscal capacity, 50% of the IRPF, IVE, tax yielded, therefore something that envys Austria; Bavaria has been asking for this type of competence for a long time. And there has been less progress in the cooperative aspects of the autonomic system. First of all, in the legislative field, with the ever postponed reform of the Senate, which is not the territorial representation chamber of article 69 of the Constitution, and in the field of collaboration between executives, because this is a body of collaboration between governments, therefore, at the executive level, where they act and then explain, and do not come to say that what they are going to do is a coordinating body of executive tasks; for it said that in the executive field, the operation of the Conference of Presidents has always been hampered. And let us remember that it was in this House, in the debate of the state of autonomy of 1992, when this proposal was made by President Fraga of the People's Parliamentary Group; it was reiterated in 1994; it was taken to the General Committee of the Autonomous Communities of the Senate in March 1997; it was reiterated in 2004; the People's Party, through the Minister Posada, tried to enter the Law on Public Administrations during his term of office — exactly in 2003, if I do not recall — and was frustrated by the opposition, at that moment headed by the socialists, and, above all, by the nationalists. Well, the fact is that state legislation is finally incorporated into the reform that the People's Party of Law 40/2015, the legal regime of the public sector, which in article 146 states that the Conference of Presidents is the first body of State cooperation, and hence the sectoral conferences are coordinated. At the same time, it is added that sectoral conferences can be convened, rather than before, by the law of 1992, on the sole initiative of the Government, at the initiative of a third of the autonomous communities. If they are also maintained for common matters, only between the Autonomous Community and the State, the bilateral committees, which join the Joint Transfers Commission, close the fundamental body which has been able to promote cooperation at the executive level. There's certainly the legislative's pending. And the 6th Conference of Presidents actually had very good results. First, it was said here that there was a lot of noise and little walnuts. Well, the 6th Conference of Presidents was the walnut that most nuts gave, there were 11 agreements. The one you've had the most backward must go back to 2009, which had four agreements. And that of 2004 and 2007, 3 agreements. So it's true that the agreements are what they are, but it's also true that there was 11, that is, much more than twice as long as there were more agreements at a president's conference. And, of course, the Conference of Presidents is a conference that serves to cooperate. It was said here that it serves, they asked the president what it was to start. To boot is to extract with root violence, and if we're talking about a body of cooperation, I think we have to go to collaborate, to cooperate, to dialogue and to reach agreements. Of course, going to a conference of presidents to start anything, because it's not the right thing to do. For that, of course, it's better not to go, which is what the two most nationalistic presidents in the autonomous communities have done. Because we must remember that, as a recently stated professor of constitutional law: "Independenceists abhor multilateral mechanisms of cooperation between autonomys such as vampires at ease." And it is certainly not understood from a nationalistic logic which is multilateral cooperation and which is even understood as an attempt to recentralise, when it is just the opposite. Precisely, it's not centralized when one, with its own competences, what it does is to deal with how it exercises these powers to achieve common objectives. Finally, we believe it is good news, which is reinforced by the second positive aspect, which is the high degree of agreements reached. 3 of an institutional nature: what reaches the conference itself; what reaches the participation of the autonomous communities in the formation of the state will in the European Union, strengthening the Committee on EU-related Affairs, coordinating the sectors with regard to Spain's participation in the European Union; and the third, the mentioned on self-financing. Another agreement on employment, to enhance and improve information, active policies and, therefore, the possibility of attaining work, and boosting dual vocational training, which reinforces the possibility of improving regulatory regulations in order to facilitate business movement in the territory and improve efficiency. And two public service agreements: civil protection, digital administration and strengthening of public services, seeking to extract the replacement rate to give stability and sustainability to public services. And five out of eleven are social affairs. In education, participate in the reform of the education pact at the state level through the Senate; set up the social card in collaboration with the third sector; encourage vulnerable consumers of electricity by coordinating with companies between administrations; against gender violence, receiving contributions in the Senate, too, for a state policy against gender violence; and demography, to seek not only a national strategy, but to bring the issue to the European Union. The balance sheet is promising in the institutional sphere of public services, employment and also in the social field, and Galicia has achieved its objectives. The first with demographics, of course, putting it at the forefront of national politics. And the second in relation to the system of self-financing. The agreement on autonomous financing is called "the sustainability of the state of well-being and self-financing". So first, the priority is to improve self-financing in order to maintain the welfare state, which is to recognize that the welfare state is supported by autonomous communities. And the indissoluble union between sustainability and self-financing and the welfare state. And it's done with principles that since Galicia, and the experts say so, are the most important. We have achieved three achievements in that agreement on financing. First, the method, the multilaterality. That it didn't happen as it happened in 2009, that Galicia had twenty-four hours of the autonomic financing agreement on the Fiscal and Financial Policy Council the document that had to take either you or you, or paid the 2,500 million of the negative liquidations inherited from the previous Government. Secondly, because the principle of solidarity is maintained and the principle of effective cost is actually introduced according to the provision of the service in each autonomous community. We are not talking about per capita financing, nor are we talking — as did the nationalists and as introduced in the past reform — about the principle of ordinality. Each community receives what it needs to give a level of public service provision and does not rework that after that so that some who were down pass up and others who are left over pass down. We have also achieved a specific treatment for health, which accounts for 40% of the cost of providing services, which means that differential costs will be treated, such as aging or even dispersal, and also introduces dependence to ensure their sustainability with contributions from the state. If we add that fiscal co-responsibility is maintained and that local funding is addressed or reformed, I believe that these are important agreements. I believe, Mr. Leicega, that it is a mistake to ask for figures as an objective in this kind of negotiations. What is needed is to achieve good mechanisms to ensure that public services are sustainable in future according to a number of principles of efficiency and effectiveness.

I compare, after the appearance that has just taken place by the President of the Union, beginning with the round of appearances that will have from now on, appearances, on the other hand, usual at the beginning of the legislation of the members of the Government of Galicia to expose the lines of action of the various councils of the Union of Galicia in the legislation we are beginning. However, this third legislation — therefore, the third appearance of general lines — is in the spirit — I think it must never be lacking — with the sense of responsibility and, of course, with the willingness to collaborate and receive all the necessary collaboration in the search for consensus, where possible, to implement the policies of my department. I think this arrangement has led to the culmination in the past legislate some features I think relevant, promoted from this department. And I therefore insist that this provision is again the same at the beginning of this legislation. I'm going to structure my intervention around two large axes — by systematizing it a little — which summarizes the actions that will take place from the Vice-Presidentship and from its different management centres. The first will be on the basis of providing public services entrusted to our responsibility, within the best possible resources and standards also suitable to meet the needs of our citizens and thus to provide quality public services. And also the second axis will consist of achieving an accessible administration, a government prepared for the new technologies and the possibilities they offer, a transparent but also efficient and always respectful administration with the resources it manages and the rights of citizens. Starting with the first of the two major axes that I have just announced, the reference to the management of the public services that we have entrusted to the Vice-Presidentship, and also starting with the issue of justice, it must be said that this legislation will allow the completion of very important projects already started in the previous one, apart from those that we are going to do with an ex-new character in this legislation. First, there will be an end — in any case, very soon — to the large judicial infrastructure already under way in the plan that began in 2010, to large legal infrastructures for construction of new buildings or, if not, major reforms of buildings that already existed. Already this month of February, in just thirty days, will complete the adaptation and reconstruction of the tobacco factory in the Coruña to allocate there the legal units of the Coruña, part of them — 16 million investment —. It's over in just a month. In the first half of 2018, next year, the works that are already being carried out in the new judicial building of Parda in Pontevedra will be completed with nearly 20 million euros of investment. And in 2019 it will complete the reform of the General Hospital of Vigo to make it a new city of Vigo Justice, with more than 25 million investments. This same year the works will begin. They must also be added, during this legislation, the construction of other works, not of such great magnitude, but of works with an important investment, in those judicial parties where necessary, where urgency or necessity makes it necessary to commit new judicial infrastructures. It's Tui's case, announced last year. The planned investment is EUR 2.2 million and this year will begin the works. And so during the legislation in which we believe, according to the High Court of Justice, that such investments are necessary. Besides, without in any case discarding minor works, small investments, but which are sometimes very necessary, in judicial buildings. We will continue to invest amounts around €500,000 annually, in addition to the Energy Efficiency Plan, with almost 3 million euros, which is a four-year plan that has already begun to run in the past legislation, and which will allow to perform works related to energy efficiency but also important works in many Jewish buildings in Greece. And so, from 2009 until today, counting on the bids that I've just exposed them, you can say that this judicial infrastructure plan — finished or, if not, geared towards ending the dates that I told you in its entirety — has allowed you to invest around 100 million euros, which is almost twice as much as you invested in judicial infrastructure in Galicia since the Autonomous Community had assumed jurisdiction over justice in 1995. Secondly, and also in the wake of justice, I believe that in the coming years, in this legislation, it must be in which we achieve among all — this is a complex task, a work that has long begun and that, of course, will need the collaboration of all those involved — that the judicial procedure is completely digitized. Following the path plan will be fundamental to this goal, and we will extend the possibility of electronic communication with the judges, the possibility of filing claims and written electronically, addressed to the Administration of Justice, from the notaries, from the penitentiaries, from hospitals, from the State Corps and Security Forces and, ultimately, and to finish, from all natural and legal persons who write to the Administration of Justice. We will also advance, in the use of the incorporation of new features, in the viewer of the legal file, which is already being implemented, it is already working, we must continue to develop it — we have been pioneers — and for this we will be very supportive of the Greek electronic judicial headquarters, in whose implementation we have also been pioneers. We will complete the video recording network, through the eFidelius system, which already covers 85% of today's rooms, far ahead of the average deployment in other autonomous communities, and will also complete the installation of the video conferencing system in all judicial units in Galicia, which is already around 30 judicial parties. We're going to complete its implementation, and we're also going very far ahead of the average deployment of these kinds of devices in other autonomous communities, and we're definitely going to do it because their advantages are obvious. We will also continue with the renewal of computer equipment, in the workplaces and in the judicial bodies, which is also necessary, first, because they have a determined useful life and they need to be solved, and more now that we want to do all these implants that I just mentioned, which need new computer equipment these jobs. And thirdly, I think it is essential that we take the final steps towards the implementation of the new judicial office, which is very closely linked, in addition to the advances in the field of new technologies in the administration of justice. So, until now this has conditioned its implementation, and once it's published, sorry, that the decree of the new office is already published, let's start, as a pilot experience, with the implementation of the judicial office in Ourense, we will then follow with the High Court of Justice, we will follow through Santiago, Lugo, Ferrol, Pontevedra, so through the cities, and, finally, we will do it in all the judicial parties. Along with investments in infrastructure, alongside investments in new technologies, no doubt it is the work of people that remains fundamental, and therefore we continue with the program of personal resources to sustain the functioning of judicial units, assuming the commitment to equip, without any doubt, all those judicial units that are being created along this legislation. Already in 2017, as soon as the Ministry publishes the decree, the judicial units approved in 2016 will begin to function, and this will assume that 24 new officials and officials will be incorporated into these newly created judicial bodies, and, of course, the Board will assume the budgetary cost that this entails, just as it will be done with all those judicial bodies — I insist — that they agree to create during the rest of the legislation. I say this because there are other autonomous communities that are giving up — or at least they did so when judicial bodies were set up — the creation of new judicial bodies, precisely because they cannot afford these budgetary costs. The Galician Union has already done so, and it will continue to do so. We will also, of course, continue with a plan that is of fundamental importance — and it is increasingly being seen as it progresses — which is the disconnection in judicial archives trials through a purge program, and this is linked to the extension of electronic file management, which is already being implemented, and which, no doubt, is combined with the elimination of many thousands of boxes, which account for many hundreds of thousands of judicial files -- this allows, first, to release space, which was becoming a pressing problem in many judicial units, and, moreover, to computerize the files so that they can be easily managed. We will continue, too, in the issue of justice by betting on the mediation system. It just celebrated the day of mediation, and I believe that the bet that has been made these years — less of drive and deployment — shows that there is already in all the cities of Greece in one way or another, sometimes working with universities and others collaborating with the schools of lawyers and prosecutors themselves; in all cases with universities looking for some kind of partner, so say, to implement mediation. It is already in the seven cities of Galicia and its future development that we expect is the need — and we will do so — to establish an administrative infrastructure from the Galician Union in order to be able to manage the mediation activity, which I believe — and we are going to bet on that — that we will experience an important development in this legislature. We will also end up with a problem that was actually being reported by the Galician Institute of Legal Medicine. It was necessary to increase the equipment, and this has led us — as you know, since I announced it in the presence of budgets — to the creation of 14 new squares in the psychosocial teams and 4 forensic medical squares, which will undoubtedly result in the agileization of the issuance of reports by this institute. And finally, by ending up in the issue of justice, in the shift of office, after these years, where we have agreed with the seven schools of lawyers a speeding up in the average times of collection, at last this year we can fulfill the commitment to raise the shift by 10%. Now we have to negotiate them, but no doubt the twenty-two days on average that are taking, from certification to schools, the school professionals to charge the shift of duty are reinforced by this 10% increase in the salaries that professionals of the profession will be receiving as early as this year 2017. Another of the key priorities will continue to be equality, the promotion of equality from the administration, and the fight against gender-based violence. I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that we have made progress in recent years. I believe that, in fact, several of the commitments made four years ago at a meeting like this, with regard to these issues, have been reached. Without a doubt, this can't lead us unless we think about the need to keep working, because of course there's a lot left to do. We are particularly pleased that we have finally put in place — that is an open path and that we must persevere in this legislation — the need for awareness that a special effort must be made in education with regard to the promotion of the values of equality and the fight against gender violence. I believe that the implementation, at last, of the last year of the Italian Plan of Education and Equality, which I have assumed, since we could begin to offer the centres a matter of gender equality, has left us a way in which we will persevere in this legislation. It has concluded the VI Women's and Men's Equality Plan, 300 actions, and what is now going to be presenting the VII plan, which is going to schedule these actions until 2020. This is about general planning, but there are also specific problems that can also be covered by these plans, but that need more timely action; therefore, we will continue to do them, especially as regards conciliation and co-responsibility. We are drawing up, and we will also present this year, the first plan of conciliation and co-responsibility of Galicia, the 2018-2022 temporal horizon, and of course, also during its elaboration, and then before its presentation, we look forward to listening to all those involved and counting on the arrivals of parliamentary groups. Another plan, to which we will continue to give special impetus in the coming years, is that of extending training — and in this we are also pioneers in all fields, in all fields, in all sectors, to all officials and civil servants working in the Administration of Galicia — specific in the area of equality, prevention and treatment of gender violence. In the coming weeks we will proceed to pass a decree that will finally extend it to all public administration staff, and we will be, as I said before, the first community to develop a regulation of this silence and with this intention. Also on its day was this pioneer Parliament in the adoption, on 14 April, of Law 2/2014, Law for equal treatment and non-discrimination of lesbians, gays, transsexuals and intersexuals, and what happens from now on — and it has already been said at that time when this law was passed — the great challenge is the development of the measures which are understood in this law in a cross-sectional manner as regards the German Government, and so we will continue these measures and in support of the LGTBI associative tissue. Ladies and gentlemen, I simply mean, and the President has already done so in his speeches — I think that in all those who have taken the floor in the appearance that has come to pass — in the offer to this House — this afternoon we will try to be able to achieve a consensus position, a joint position, to move the position of the Galician Parliament, and through this position of the Galician Parliament to the position of the people of Greece, to the specific session that will take place in the Senate, after the Conference of Presidents, on measures to combat gender violence. I believe that we must all be there, overcoming the different points of view that we may have, in a joint position; I think it would be good for everyone, and therefore by the Government of Greece there remains the disposition which the President of the Union has already expressed a moment ago: I believe that the Committee on Equality would, after making the necessary debates, be the appropriate body to be able to carry out this position. With regard to the victims of gender-based violence, we will also present in the coming weeks the protocol for coordination and institutional cooperation against gender-based violence, which seeks nothing more than to give, clearly and concisely, victims of gender-based violence a tool that provides them with personalized, specialized attention, and that provides them with a clear itinerary in their dealings with administrations, that they always have a reference technical person, and therefore that it is all the easier for them, within the normally difficult situation that they are supposed to be victims of gender-based violence, that dealing with administrations does not suppose an added penalty. Of course, we will also maintain direct support for the victims of gender-based violence, for the people who depend on them as well, for the economic aid. This year, as you know, we've increased regular arrivals by more than 60%, we'll continue to provide them with free psychological assistance, of course everything they need, and this year we've increased the budget allocation -- as they know and I said in the budget appearance -- by 23%. And we will follow and continue — and all of this I have just said, of course, will be throughout the legislation — with work programmes specific to victims of gender violence. And all of this as resources improve, maintain and continue to extend the resources of proximity through, above all, the local administrations, which is often the first door to which victims of gender-based violence approach; and all of this will logically do so, apart from specific programs directed at the councils, at local coordination tables in the field of prevention and combating gender-based violence, through, specifically, from information centres to women. The Galician Union will continue to make an important bet for its maintenance in collaboration with the municipalities, and for its extension where there really is a clear will on the part of the local authorities, who still do not have them, to have these resources of first necessity. Also, before moving on to the interior matter, and related to gender-based violence, you know that it came into operation last summer the specific unit of the Police assigned to the fight against gender-based violence. So, during this legislation, once it's been in operation -- I say it's only been half a year -- it will further improve its coordination with the rest of the State Corps and Security Forces, and, therefore, it will become a unit, we hope that essential and fundamental, to help the victims. Referring to the police unit—after making this reference to this new role that has been developed for six months—to say that we will continue its empowerment, not only for this fundamental work that they do in the attention of victims of gender violence, but also for another related to theft, the protection of minors, the protection of the environment and the heritage. That's why we're still betting on this unit and this way of having a police force attached to the Galician Union, and that's why we're going to keep insisting that it needs to be completed again. It is true that after the incorporations that have occurred in recent years we have reached almost – and that is the ideal goal – 500, but they have lost force and that is why we will ask them to complete the Ministry of the Interior, being aware that this is an important effort, since also the national police staff itself is demanding that it be completed, but we believe that we have sufficient reasons to make this claim. So much less the situation is what it was like when in 2009 we found the police unit with just 350 troops, but it's true that we want to get closer to the ideal ratio of 500. At the same time, we are looking at — and we will do it throughout this legislation — its material provision with a virtually complete renewal of the attached police car park, which is very old-fashioned, and we will also start with the inauguration, last week, precisely, of the new headquarters that will have the police unit inscribed in the city of Coruña, with a reorganization of the headquarters of this unit, both in Vigo — where I think it is the most necessary — as in the city of Ourense. This refers to the police unit. I would also like to pretend that in this legislation we want to make a boost, to increase collaboration as regards the functioning of the local police. The Galician Union has clear powers defined in the law on the regulation of local police, which are a municipal body — as everyone knows — but we have coordination powers. So here we will also have three main objectives: firstly, in the development of the law of the year 2007, of coordination — particularly what I was referring to earlier — which has just been changed in other respects. We believe, and I believe, that it is very necessary — and many mayors and mayors are demanding it — to pass a decree that collects inter-municipal cooperation as far as possible. It's true that here we are very conditioned also by the Law of State Security Forces and Bodies and by other regulations. But it is true that it must be possible, and it must be made possible by regulation, that municipal police can collaborate and do things outside their own municipal term; or, at least, when there are several local police performing joint actions. I say that, legally, it's not a simple issue, there are a number of obstacles that must be overcome, but I think we have to be able to do it, because this — in the end — is optimizing resources, and often means giving a council a solution that sometimes cannot be achieved by simply turning to the state's forces and security bodies that are located in its county or its area. This will also lead us to establish joint action protocols of the various local police bodies, respecting the municipal autonomy of every county with a police force. We believe that there must be joint protocols of action, especially if we want these forms of approval, which I was referring to earlier. And finally, it has already been announced at the meeting of the president of the board with the president of the Fegamp — if the municipalities so wish — that there is a framework agreement that we will sign almost immediately with the Fegamp to make it possible. But then it'll have to be the city halls that ask you -- in a number, in addition, enough to be able to convene these selective processes -- to the Galician Council that centralize the local police's selective evidence, so it's a single test with all those men and women who want to access these bodies. And from there on, let's make -- so to speak -- a single list of people who go beyond the selective processes so they can go to the different municipalities. I insist that this is a complaint from many municipalities, simply the Council of Galicia, what it expresses here is the possibility and willingness to do so in this legislature. I insist, as long as the municipalities, in sufficient numbers, decide to entrust this task to the self-government. And the groups of those rapid-intervention municipalities, which were later derived from the GHGs — which is a much more stable structure — but the reinforcement — especially in the last two years — of the troops and the civil protection material, through volunteer associations and more than 200 municipalities, which logically leads to a map of availability of emergency personnel very different from what was when the law of the year 2007 was passed, the Emergency Act. It is therefore necessary in this legislation to develop an amendment to this law that takes into account — I say — that status quo is very different; fortunately, because we have more effective, more medium and more professional than we had been ten-five years ago. I don't think in recent years the time has been lost, much less. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u128	G.P. motion. of the Galego Nationalist Block, at the initiative of Ms. Ana Belén Pontón Mondelo.
