ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u129	To formulate the motion you have the floor to Mrs. Pontón Mondelo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u130	Thank you very much. Good afternoon to all and to all. Today the Galego Nationalist Block brings to this plenary a serious, ambitious proposal with concrete contents so that we actually put in place a Greek pact against gender violence. In this sense, I want to highlight something that is important to us: to speak of a Greek covenant against gender-based violence is not to speak of a label, not of empty words to cover consciences or to do what is done, but for us it is only possible to make a Greek covenant against gender-based violence if there are more means, more resources and really new initiatives to fight against male violence. Because the truth is that women are tired of seeing how while women are murdered the only thing there are good words, great phrases, but then, in reality, in what is within our hands, in what we can do from the scope of public institutions, we see how there is a certain resistance to moving, walking and assuming that there are new strategies. Because, of course, we are facing a very serious problem, one of the most extended and socially consented human rights violations. This must therefore be a matter of concern, of concern, and of what this Government's empty silons do, because they also offend us in the debate on this initiative. We're talking about violence and I want to remember that talking about gender-based violence is murder. According to the official data of the ministry — I will not collect the data published by Feminicide but the official data of the ministry, which are sufficiently serious — since 2003 more than eight hundred women have been murdered in the Spanish State. At that same stage, 50 women were murdered in Galicia, 6.3 percent of the women killed in the state as a whole. But, obviously, murders are the cruelest, most extreme face of violence, but they're not the only face of violence. We know that every year there are 5,000 women who file complaints in the Greek courts, but at the same time, more optimistic studies than they are warning us is that complaints do not reach as much as 20% of the actual cases of gender-based violence in our country. So we only see the tip of the iceberg. And we don't want to forget that behind every complaint, behind every blow, there's a woman who's suffering and a woman who needs us to be next to her. Because today there are thousands of women who, when they open the door to their homes, don't feel safe and happy, but go straight to hell. And today in this debate we would like to give a voice to some of those witnesses so that we don't forget that we don't talk about something cold but we talk about something real that affects us as humans: Mary was a victim of male violence for four years and reports that the problem came when verbal and physical arguments and assaults began. He says, "You're not aware of anything. One day, suddenly, he wore a knife in his hand because he took another one. And after four years, which seemed forty, I decided to react: I went to the hospital, I reported and I had a quick trial, so they imposed a restraining order on him. However, the law was not enough, because he jumped this order several times and yet they reduced the time from three years to one year." Antonia tells us in an interview: "Violence always goes further. Behind the first cry comes the phrase that condemns each conversation: you're stupid. At first I was sorry and two months later I would build up tension and scream again and hit the wall. I used to be quiet again because of panic. I had no work, I couldn't leave my house, I had nowhere to go and yes, I was very, very scared. Until the day came when I was much more scared of what I was going to do when I got back to work. I grabbed my daughter, got in the car and left. It was the best I could do because then I knew he was walking with a machete looking for me. A woman living domestic violence remains fragmented. You recognize it is painful because you actually accept and allow someone to beat you up and mistreat you. It's like a death in life." I, I don't know you, but every time I hear these or other stories we can see fortunately through the media, because I keep thinking about how many Marias, how many Antonias, how many women we will never be able to recognize or name today in this Parliament, they felt the same as Mary and Antonia. But I also think of all those women who will never be able to tell us their stories, because they were women who were buried and murdered because they said they were their mates and they would want them all their lives. And today I think we have a debt pending with many women, women like Ana Gómez Nieto, women like Ana María Enjamio Carrillo, women like Elena Michaela Marcu, who were murdered last year in our country. And I really think we have to not stay in the conformism that everything is fine, that there is nothing to do, and I really think we have to do something else and that we have to put this as a subject where we are really willing to do something more than say good words that end up falling into a broken bag. I would like this to be not a repetition of this speech today: demagogic, populist, which reduces machismo and all its consequences to absurdity and even to prevent us from being able to talk about it. I'm not going to disenfranchise anyone because no one has granted me those powers; I don't know if in the intrusion that has a side of the bench right now they already believe in the right to tell us what we can talk about. In any case, we are not going to reduce this debate to a pim pam pom absurd partisan and that, clearly, the only thing that introduces us is a strategy that is absolutely useless at improving the situation. Clearly, what has been done so far has been all important. But, in view of what is happening in our country, we sincerely say that everything is insufficient, that there is a resounding of violence, that there is a new neo-machist strategy and that we have an obligation to review what has not been done, what is working wrong and the things we can improve. Because if you don't, it seems like we're in a kind of roupy where what hasn't been done before can't be done now. I refuse to accept that dynamic because, among other things, we would continue in prehistory, in the caverniculum, and I think it would be important for dialectics to be given in other terms. We understand that one thing is the debate that must be produced in the state and another is the debate that we have to have in Galicia in relation to our own country's competences. And in these terms is where we want to place a proposal for a Greek covenant against gender violence in which the Galician Union, all this chair of advisers and advisers who today do not agree to this debate, because they should assume their responsibilities because they have in their hands the ability to improve the political, social and institutional response against gender violence. And in that sense we make ten concrete proposals: some are directly dependent on the Union; elsewhere we want to do a common front from Galicia in order to be able to defend improvements in the response of the judicial or institutional system to such a serious situation as that which causes gender violence. The first thing we think about is that more resources are needed; more resources are needed not only for the assistance of women who are in a situation of gender-based violence, but because there is a lack of training and co-responsibility, because more means and more resources are needed to make this really a political priority. We've recorded that the goal must be that we have at least 1% of the budget dedicated to fighting against gender violence. We can talk about how much or how little it seems to them, whether we want to program it, but there has to be a real commitment in this direction. The second, to us, it's important to categorize well what we're talking about. There's a new category that helps us define better what's going on with gender-based violence: feminicide. We propose that this be included in the Welsh legislation and that a status of the victim of feminicide be put in place as well, which coordinates the entire response to women who suffer from gender-based violence. Thirdly, to improve the resources of justice, both with more judges and with the staffing they have to have. And here at this point, we have gathered proposals which the Galician Council of Lawyers has put forward in the non-permanent Commission on the equality we have in this Parliament, which has made proposals which remain there. We collect those proposals. We think we need to work on education, so we think it would be important to take a step forward as a Welsh law of co-education. We think it's important that more preventive measures exist. I would like to stop — if I may have a second — in an aspect that is relevant, which is what it has to do with psychological care: the Greek Government is failing to comply with the law, but, aside from this — that we say that compliance with the law, which says that psychological attention should be considered as a primary care service, we understand that, as not everything is going to be done from day to morning, the programs that are working have to be opened to all women. And precisely the women who haven't broken up with violence can be very important for them to take that step and there's psychological support...

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u132	...and so all the applications open up in that sense. We believe that the treatment of this kind of aid that allows women to be autonomous and solvent, to focus on seeking a job of their training, should be treated with all urgency. We all understand that there is a need for formality in the processing of a benefit, but let's look at the case of a patient entering an Emergency Service with a serious bleeding wound. No doctor would ask to cover a print to the patient before attending and put it into surgery. We all understand that. For this is — we think — the degree of sensitivity and responsibility that the Government should have with these women. Even more so when these laws regulating aid already envisage the return, in case of non-compliance with the conditions, before, during or at the end of the benefit. They give priority, therefore, to the provision and claim as soon as they have to claim prints that prevent women like this, who meet all the requirements, from being deprived of resources for months on a purely formal issue. The delay of these kinds of issues is the reason why women are suspicious of the guarantees of protection that are announced as such. In the motion for amendment, the Socialists also request the establishment of specific intervention protocols for full-time care for women who have withdrawn the complaint for gender violence, as well as a personalized judicial accompaniment to make information available to women on the itinerary and safer procedure in their judicial journey from the moment the complaint is filed to the end of the process. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u140	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. Mrs. Pontón, Mrs. Pierres, for more than 20 years I started working with women victims of gender-based violence and I know many Marias, many Anas, Victoria, Lucia, Virtudes; I could tell them many cases. What I do perceive is that since I started working with these women until today things have changed a lot, fortunately. Legal protection, resources, instruments, awareness of society is essential. But there's one before and one after, and the one before and after I always put it in order of protection. That has to lead us to a reflection: since we have the order of protection, there's a falling resource, which is the foster home. So sometimes it's not so important to put the emphasis on the resource because there are sometimes resources that, like that one, are solved with other issues. So sometimes you have to use a little more common sense than having resources. It's true that there are resources that are essential, but it's also true that there are other resources that have fallen into disuse precisely because of the legislation and because other instruments that have been more powerful to the aggressor, which have been more powerful to the batterer, which have been more powerful to the killer, allow women to have better comfort. Because, where is that woman better at her home or in a foster home? At your house. Well, we have to be able to have fewer resources among all, but the woman has more comfort. And that's the goal we all have to mark. Where do we have a very serious problem? We have a very serious problem with education, ladies and gentlemen. Why? People of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr César Manuel Fernández Gil and four other Members, on the approval by the Government of Galicia of the rule governing the recruitment regime of the staff labour investigator of the Public Service of Galicia
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u174	This motion for a non-law amendment by the Socialist Parliamentary Group was tabled.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u175	To formulate the non-law proposition Mr. Blanco Paradelo has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u176	Good afternoon, gentlemen. We want to speak with this initiative of the pioneer Galicia, but of the pioneer Galicia in a positive way, as I will try to expose myself to the introduction. The developments in research and innovation in recent years have made it necessary to develop new regulatory frameworks in order to achieve greater momentum in this area. In December 2013, the Council of the European Union formally adopted the new rules, the legislation it will govern in the European Union for the period 2014-2020, in order to make the European economy a smart, sustainable and inclusive economy. Within the action to achieve this objective, and as a follow-up to the previous framework programmes, the Horizon 2020 programme was approved, spending around EUR 80 million for the period 2014-2020, with the aim of bringing the ideas of laboratories to markets, eliminating barriers to innovation and facilitating collaboration between the public and private sectors, in order to ensure Europe's overall competitiveness and sustainable growth, placing special emphasis on scientific excellence and industrial leadership towards the welfare state. The ahead program includes regional strategies for smart specialization, known as the RIS3, and we have to say that the Galician community was the first in the state to propose its RIS3 strategy and submit it to the European Union authorities, obtaining a great welcome and support, which allowed it to launch almost immediately. In addition, he did so through a participatory process, coordinated by the Galega Innovation Agency, in which all the agents of the Galician Innovation System intervened, from the different departments of the autonomous administration itself to citizenship, through other institutions and economic and social agents, publicly presenting on 25 March 2014. Ladies and gentlemen, the Government of Greece has already been able to help universities, technology centres and companies in Greece to raise funds for the first three years, 2014, 2015 and 2016, of the programme — as I said — Horizon 2020, which claimed a return of more than 41 million euros, which almost tripled that achieved in the same period of the previous programme in 2007, 2008 and 2009. In the autonomic field, Law 5/2013, of 30 June, on the promotion of Galician research and innovation, in which the Greek Research and Innovation Plan was developed, as a fundamental tool for planning and coordinating research, transfer, valorisation and innovation policies in Galicia, also known as Plan I2C, or even the Opportunius program for the incorporation and return of researchers, or the Principle program. But we, far from conforming, want to achieve more, and we know that much more needs to be done. Last week, in Commission 6a, we approved an initiative in which we proposed calling on the Galician Summit to encourage the necessary measures to increase the collection of Community funds for R & D+i by the Greek entities, with particular attention to small and medium-sized enterprises, within the framework of existing European funding programmes for these activities, by hearing the opposition groups to reach broad consensus in the momentum of the R & D in Galicia, as we are also doing today with this initiative, which seeks only to speed up, encourage the adoption as soon as possible of a rule that regulates the recruitment regime of labour investigators of the Autonomous Community Administration of Galicia. Ladies and gentlemen, Galicia must continue to make an additional effort to address the challenge of its development and its consolidation and internationalization, as it was starting from a position below the average of the autonomous communities and regions of the European Union. In a context of economic crisis like ours, Galicia was able to do more with less. And, as I said before, it was from the state's first autonomous communities to propose its RIS strategy, setting out priority lines of action that include twenty instruments, among which the retention, incorporation and mobility programme of talent, as we were pioneers, and I would like to remind you, of the adoption of Galicia's Telecommunications and Infrastructure Act, or even what the vice president said this morning at the electronic judicial headquarters. Therefore, taking into account the Strategic Plan of Galicia and the need to develop Law 14/2011, of June 1, of science, technology and innovation, we intend to promote a standard that will re-place Galicia in the head, being pioneers in Spain to regulate the employee's employment regime that performs his work in research centres of the Autonomic Administration, a staff that can be hired through the public bodies of the Galician Union that develop their scientific work in the fields of innovation and health for a period of four or five years, depending on the nature of the contract. This draft decree responds to the need to address the challenge of consolidating the development and internationalization of our Autonomous Community and the importance of promoting research to achieve a more competitive market economy and enabling greater job creation. What is intended, ladies and gentlemen, is to ensure that a new legal framework can be established which takes into account the importance of human capital with greater training towards progress and the improvement of the quality of life of citizens, which takes into account the repeated demands of members of the Spanish foreign scientific group and others who are sensitive to these — as I say — demands. So, ladies and gentlemen, the Galician Union will for the first time create the figure of the investigator's staff and will include it in the autonomic administration's employment catalogue, with the aim of favoring the retention of scientific talent in our community. And, of course, the selection of these staff should be based on the principles of equality, public competition and competitiveness, taking into account for this selection the different regulations and procedures for the selection of teaching staff and researchers from universities and the Higher Council of Scientific Research (CSIC); since, therefore, it is necessary to establish objective criteria and reflect excellence in the tasks of R & D+i carried out by the aspirants. We are also, of course, convinced that this decree should contemplate the working conditions of the hired investigator, as well as the schedules, permits, vacations, pay, duration and extension of contract time, and should also consider, whenever possible, the possibility of carrying out training stays in other R & D+i centres and transfer of technology to others and prestige. For the above reasons, therefore, we would like to introduce this initiative to you in order for the Galician Parliament to urge the Union to adopt the rule governing the recruitment regime of staff labour investigators of the Galician Autonomous Community Administration. Today we have been listening here all day appealing the different groups to the consensus, to the agreement. So today I — just like we did the other day at the committee — also appeal, ladies and gentlemen, to a great deal of agreement on something as important as it is to retain talent and to bet on this case for the research quality of our young people and our girls. Nothing else and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u257	Thank you very much. G.P.'s non-legislative proposal. de En Marea, at the initiative of D. Luis Villares Naveira and D. Antón Sánchez García, on the impetus for the Galician Union to comply with the judgment of 21 December 2016 of the European Union Court of Justice in relation to mortgage contracts with ground clauses
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u258	This motion for a non-law has been tabled.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u259	Mr. Antón Sánchez has the floor to make a non-law proposal.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u260	The Court of Justice of the European Union, in its judgment of 21 December 2016, has just failed in favour of consumers in the case of compensation for unfair clauses, abusive clauses. Credit institutions will have to return everything customers have over-burdened during the life of the mortgage loan and not only from May 9, 2013, as the Supreme Court had established. According to the Court of Justice of the European Union, Spanish jurisprudence, which limits in time the effects of the declaration of nullity of the ground clauses contained in mortgage lending contracts, is incompatible with Union law. This limitation leads to incomplete and insufficient consumer protection, so it does not constitute an adequate and effective means to stop the use of abusive clauses. In this judgment it is stated that an abusive legal declaration of a clause must result in the restoration of the situation in which the consumer would find himself if there was no such clause. The consequence, therefore, is that they are returned incorrectly charged by the existence of interest-free ground clauses, with interest included. This judgment and the consequences on ground clauses is one more example of the continuous treatment of Spanish governments with the bank for years, in different colors, it has to be said, which has also led to a situation of abuse of consumers, many years, all the time. Once again, as I said before, another scam on the big scale, too, allowed in convenance with those governments. And once again, in this case, the European authorities who have to come out in defence of consumer rights paradoxically, and put in color, red to those who instead hold other positions, such as the Supreme Court judgment, that even one of the arguments for the irretroactivity of ground clauses was that there could be a serious change in economic order. I mean, the priority is to protect the banks once more. For years the Spanish State has been setting up a system that constantly assumed not only abuse of people, but constantly violate Community consumer protection legislation. This was supposed to be billions of euros stolen. The European Court of Justice has already declared that mortgage legislation, on matters as sensitive as the donation paid, protection against the refugees, was contrary to European legislation. And always the tug and the ghastly, Spanish governments were changing the rules that imposed them from Europe, not on their own initiative, which were always on the bank's side. Now the ground clauses, but also the costs of formalizing mortgages, but now there are up to fifteen clauses in question that can be abusive, maybe! And the whole system of the last twenty, twenty-five years in question, is scandalous, and once again there were political leaders, the successive governments that have constantly legislated in favour of the bank and that have tolerated this situation. As I said, the judgment states that the legal declaration of an abusive nature of a clause must result in the restoration of the situation in which the consumer would be found if there was no such clause. That is to say, it is meridianly clear that the sentence requires the return of all amounts charged whenever there are abusive clauses, abusive floor clauses, all of them! In view of this situation, we say, how do we guarantee better that right of consumers who suffered these ground clauses? For we bet because extrajudicial proceedings are open with all guarantees to be returned to them — which are the rights of these consumers — without necessarily having to resort to the judicial procedure, causing more expense, more effort and more time, since they have clearly recognized that right. And that's why, initially, in our proposal we do not demand that the Board implement an extrajudicial system of these characteristics, with guarantees, and not obliged to resort to justice. Well, I also have to say that we amend ourselves, because in the middle of this time — between we register it and the debate came — the Royal Decree Act 1/2017 was passed. They have to excuse the different groups and the people who were going to come to the debate, but we believe that that self-amendment is justified, because the adoption of a basic legislation, of course, must also be met here; the Board could not initiate a procedure autonomously. Once this legislation is considered basic, what it does is to modify the proposal according to which I initially did it, but with the same idea, with the idea of providing a procedure that facilitates the payment of those amounts with all guarantees. But once again, by analysing the Royal Decree Act 1/2017, we see that within the framework defined by the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union, and within the possibilities that there is, all possibilities, this agreement uses them to benefit the bank, once again. And all the possibilities and scraps that the bank was going to benefit were used to benefit the bank, and once again the balance leans on the side of the banks. I mean, as we see in this real decree the bank informs the customer — to all customers who have ground clauses — that the customer can voluntarily file a claim, which is called a prior complaint, and from there everything is already in the hands of the bank, which becomes a judge and part. The bank can simply answer that it does not consider that the customer's clause is abusive, and it ends, puts an end to the procedure. Otherwise, the bank itself is the one that calculates the amount to be returned, sends that proposal to the client, without any mediation, without any agency expected to check whether the offer the bank is making is correct or not. The consumer must show whether he agrees with the calculation. The deadline for the agreement is three months, and the bank can do the following: it can expressly reject the consumer's request, end of proceedings; three months without communication, no customer's answer, no end of proceedings, no negative administrative silence, no need to answer; or, if not, it can even answer the bank, and if in the three months it does not enter the amount to the customer, the procedure is completed and it has no consequences for the bank itself. I mean, you give the banks three months of leave without any consequences, do whatever they do. The consumer cannot take legal action during extrajudicial proceedings, and only if the consumer rejects the bank's offer and pleads for legal action, in which he obtained a more favourable judgment than the offer received, will costs the bank. I mean, this is scandalous, too. If the bank makes an offer to the customer, the customer thinks it's not fair and then resorts to justice, if he does not receive a judgment with an amount higher than the amount offered by the bank, the costs are for the customer. If during the process the bank produces a loop, because the same thing happens, if then the amount is not more than that amount offered by the bank, the costs are also for the customer. I mean, we think it favors the bank in all these respects. Nor does he make any reference to the executed mortgages, who were thrown out of their homes, who suffered the abusive clauses, those who lost their homes for a mortgage loan that had these abusive clauses. There is no sanctioning mechanism against the bank. We must compare the zeal with the failure of consumers to comply with the tolerance of bank defaults in this country. There is no budget provided for in the real law decree, it offers no guarantees, because there is no body between the bank and the consumer, and the relationship between the bank and the customer of the bank for us is not a relationship between equal, it needs a public body, as possible — which could be the Galician Consumer Institute — to mediate and advise also, to advise, the customer in the calculation — which is not a simple calculation — of the quantities. We believe, moreover, that it is not a relationship between equal, but much less with the precedents that the bank has in this country, that is, who guarantees that there will never again be abusive clauses or abusive mechanisms about the client, who ensures that that agreement does not become a fraud to the client and that we put ourselves in another loop of scams? There's no one to guarantee it. The customer can say, well, you've accepted, but there may again be an abusive agreement for the consumer, and there's no one to guarantee that and no one to supervise. What if the bank is in breach of legislation again? We believe that all this justifies a request for the amendment of the royal decree, so we urge the Council of Galicia to demand from the Government of the State the modification, with retroactive effects, of the Royal Decree of Law 1/2017, in order to guarantee the rights of consumers, the mandatory return of...
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u261	Finish, please.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-01-24-DSPG012.u268	Thank you, Mr. President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. As already mentioned here, the Supreme Court of Spain, on May 9, 2013, issued a ruling in which ground clauses were collected as abusive and therefore null. But in the same judgment the retroactive character was not recognised, so it did not affect all those situations prior to the publication of that judgment. On 21 December 2016, a judgment by the Court of Justice of the European Union challenged this decision by the Spanish Supreme Court and established that a clause is null and void and therefore, for all purposes, with full retroactivity. On the basis of that judgment, we see that — after calculating the Bank of Spain — the Spanish bank will have to disburse some EUR 4 billion, and over EUR 200 million only in Galicia. The Greek consumer and user association estimates that in Galicia there are potentially some beneficiaries, between 50,000 and 70,000, so the figure is important. In view of this judgment, the PP has not established any return proceedings, preferring, as always, to look elsewhere. It has come back to an alarming situation — which affected a very large number of citizens — a way of dealing with problems such as those already experienced on other occasions, for example in the case of the preferences. It had to be the Socialist Party at the Congress of Deputies who had forced the debate and to take a series of measures that took place in Royal Decree Act 1/2017, published — as stated earlier — on January 21 at the BOE, that is, this Saturday, in which the bank is obliged to establish procedures for the extrajudicial claim, which returns the whole without any loss, which calculates the interest of delay incurred and which is the whole process with complete transparency. The truth is that we, after presenting the En Marea GNP, came here today to vote in favour of that GNP, since you presented a GNP in which, in your resolution, they proposed, and I say textually: "Enable and activate the necessary mechanisms and instruments of arbitration and extrajudicial solution for the return, by credit institutions, of the amounts charged to customers of mortgage contracts with ground clauses." Proposals which we socialists believe are collected, practically in their entirety, in Royal Decree Act 1/2017, and therefore we do not present any amendment to that initial GNP that you have submitted to us, since in the royal decree it is established that entities must implement a claim system, which will have a voluntary character for the consumer, to meet those affected by the accounts of the ground clause. This is set out in Article 3(1). And also, in Article 3, it states that entities must ensure that this claim system is known to all those affected, i.e. that it is addressed to all those affected to explain how they can claim their money; that those affected will charge all their money free of charge for consumers and with the maximum conditions for transparency and accountability; that the maximum time limit — as you said before — will be, at the latest, three months, and that they can pay cash to the consumer if he agrees; and that it is the consumer who decides to accept or reject the offer, and in the case of rejection of the offer, of course, the judicial route remains. In the case, too, that the bank has made a lower offer to the consumer through the legal route, you will impose the penalty on the bank itself. That's why we are surprised that you are presenting today an amendment to your own LNP in which you request the amendment of the royal decree. We're going to abstain, otherwise we're going to approve it, of course, but we're going to abstain because we understand that practically all the proposals that you make are collected. Besides, we didn't have a chance to introduce an amendment, since it was amended with a lot of precipitation. In the case that you propose, and it is the only thing we see that there can be some difference — and that they talk about compulsory return and on their own initiative — we understand that in this way the consumer himself is not allowed to decide how the said return suits him most, since it cannot be forgotten that this return carries tax obligations, which may entail a significant change in the IRPF deductions. We'll follow closely this procedure, we know it's not excellent, but if it's the best, the fastest and the most agile to avoid complicated situations and to fix most of them within three months, at most, of the three months that come set in the royal decree. We will continue to demand that a follow-up body be propelled...
